People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

And sometimes I feel like dying would be… fair, I guess. Like a punishment for what I’ve done to my family and the guys I work with. I know that probably sounds awful. I just didn’t know what else to do, so I came here.

Yeah… I guess my energy’s been pretty low. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just being lazy, because I do try to keep up.

feels connected in my head.\n- key: S022\n  name: Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior\n  description: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: At work I've caught myself pacing the same hallway with my trowel in my hand, checking the grout lines over and over, then freezing in a doorway because I felt like moving would make something bad happen.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally reason my husband got worse, the crew lost work, and clients' homes got messed up, like I poisoned everything I touched and deserve whatever happens to me.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I came in because I keep thinking it would be better if I didn't wake up, and sometimes I No… I don’t think I’ve had two whole months where it was just gone.

reason my husband got worse, the crew lost work, and clients' homes got messed up, like I poisoned everything I touched and deserve whatever happens to me.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I came in because I keep thinking it would be better if I didn't wake up, and sometimes I feel like dying would be the fair punishment for what I've done to my family and my crew.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: During the months when I was barely eating, wishing I might not wake up, and blaming myself for everything, I was also hearing that man's voice and believing the job calls and floor patterns were meant to expose me.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: For most of the time since this started, the guilt, exhaustion, loss of interest, and thoughts about death have been there, even if some weeks were less crushing than others.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations I know that sounds bad. I’m sorry.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I still try sometimes, so it’s not like I’ve completely stopped everything.\n\nBut I used to paint, and cook these big meals, and I’d run before jobs sometimes. I was also pretty good about showing up for theater rehearsals. Now all of that just feels like… chores from somebody else’s life, if that makes sense. Like I know I used to care, but I don’t really feel it anymore. And if I miss it, I just… don’t care much. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling like dying would be a fair punishment for what you've done to your family and the guys you work with, can you tell me more about what you've done that's causing you to feel so guilty?\n\nPatient: I mean… I didn’t do one huge thing, not exactly. It’s more like… everywhere I’m involved, things get worse.\n\nMy husband got worse after being around me, and at work the crew lost jobs, and then clients’ homes got messed up, and I keep thinking that’s because of me. Like I poisoned everything I touched. I know that sounds… extreme, maybe, and I’m not trying to be dramatic. But it feels true in my head, like I deserve whatever happens to me because of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how quickly you move compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess my energy’s been pretty low. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just being lazy, because I do try to keep up.\n\nBut lately, by the time I carry one box of tile or answer a couple client messages, my body feels like wet cement. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that others couldn't?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah. I’m not trying to make it bigger than it is. But yes, that has happened.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing the man's voice?\n\nPatient: I think… it’s been close to a year. Maybe around eleven months.\n\nAt first I tried to tell myself it was just stress or noise from the job site, so I didn’t really count it. But looking back, that’s about when it started… the voice, and the feeling that things were being followed through jobs or messages. I know that sounds strange. Is that the kind of timeline you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that others couldn't?\n\nPatient: Not really seeing things, no… at least not like a person or something standing there.\n\nIt’s more the voice. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, and I’m sorry if this is too much right away, but I keep having thoughts like… maybe it would be better if I just didn’t wake up.\n\nAnd sometimes I feel like dying would be… fair, I guess. Like a punishment for what I’ve done to my family and the guys I work with. I know that probably sounds awful. I just didn’t know what else to do, so I came here.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these dark thoughts?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure when it started being this bad… I think the thoughts about not waking up have been around for a while, but lately they’ve been harder to push away.\n\nI came in because I keep thinking it would be better if I just didn’t wake up, and sometimes I feel like dying would be… the fair punishment for what I’ve done to my family and my crew. I know that sounds bad. I’m sorry.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I still try sometimes, so it’s not like I’ve completely stopped everything.\n\nBut I used to paint, and cook these big meals, and I’d run before jobs sometimes. I was also pretty good about showing up for theater rehearsals. Now all of that just feels like… chores from somebody else’s life, if that makes sense. Like I know I used to care, but I don’t really feel it anymore. And if I miss it, I just… don’t care much. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling like dying would be a fair punishment for what you've done to your family and the guys you work with, can you tell me more about what you've done that's causing you to feel so guilty?\n\nPatient: I mean… I didn’t do one huge thing, not exactly. It’s more like… everywhere I’m involved, things get worse.\n\nMy husband got worse after being around me, and at work the crew lost jobs, and then clients’ homes got messed up, and I keep thinking that’s because of me. Like I poisoned everything I touched. I know that sounds… extreme, maybe, and I’m not trying to be dramatic. But it feels true in my head, like I deserve whatever happens to me because of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how quickly you move compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess my energy’s been pretty low. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just being lazy, because I do try to keep up.\n\nBut lately, by the time I carry one box of tile or answer a couple client messages, my body feels like wet cement. And my head gets so tired that even simple decisions feel hard. Like I’ll just sit there trying to figure out the next basic thing, and it’s… embarrassing. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that others couldn't?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah. I wasn’t sure if I should say that, because it sounds bad.\n\nSometimes I hear a man’s voice, like from behind me, at job sites or even in my kitchen. It’ll say things like, “You missed it,” or “Everyone knows.” It’s very clear, like someone is standing right there, but when I turn around there’s nobody there.\n\nI know that probably sounds… I don’t know. I’m not trying to make it bigger than it is. But yes, that has happened.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing the man's voice?\n\nPatient: I think… it’s been close to a year. Maybe around eleven months.\n\nAt first I tried to tell myself it was just stress or noise from the job site, so I didn’t really count it. But looking back, that’s about when it started… the voice, and the feeling that things were being followed through jobs or messages. I know that sounds strange. Is that the kind of timeline you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that others couldn't?\n\nPatient: Not really seeing things, no… at least not like a person or something standing there.\n\nIt’s more the voice. Like I said, it’ll sound like a man behind me, at work or sometimes in my kitchen, saying stuff like, “You missed it,” or “Everyone knows.” It feels very real in the moment, clear like someone’s right there, and then I turn around and nobody is. Is that still the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely happy, energetic, or irritable, and your thoughts were racing?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not like extremely happy or full of energy.\n\nI mean, I get irritable sometimes, sure, but it doesn’t feel like my thoughts are racing in some big way or that I’m suddenly on top of the world. I’m not sure if I’m answering that right, but I haven’t noticed a period like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely happy or on top of the world for a period of time?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had days where I felt okay or maybe a little better than usual, but not like “on top of the world” or anything extreme.\n\nIf anything, I’d notice if I suddenly had a big burst like that, I think. But no, I don’t believe that’s happened. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed or lost interest in most things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but for more than a couple weeks, probably longer, I just haven’t really cared about things I used to do.\n\nI used to paint, cook big meals, go running before jobs, show up for theater rehearsals… and now it all feels like chores from somebody else’s life. Like I know I used to enjoy them, but I don’t really feel pulled toward any of it, and I don’t care if I miss it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed or lost interest in most things that was significantly worse than what you've been experiencing lately?\n\nPatient: I guess… not really “worse than lately,” because lately has kind of been the same thing. It’s been pretty steady, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt better, with no depression, that lasted for at least two months since your symptoms started?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had two whole months where it was just gone.\n\nThere have been weeks that were less awful, I guess, where I could function a little better or not feel quite so weighed down. But most of the time since it started, the guilt and being exhausted and not caring about things… and, um, thoughts about death, have still been there in some form.\n\nI’m sorry, I don’t mean to sound dramatic. It’s just… I don’t think there’s been a real break from it for that long.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period of feeling depressed like this in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like this. I’ve had sad times before, sure, but this one has been the main one that really stayed.\n\nIt’s been with me for about a year and two months now, since a few months after my husband’s injury. I hope that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of feeling depressed like this before this one started?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I had a period like this before. Like I poisoned everything I touched. I know that sounds… extreme, maybe, and I’m not trying to be dramatic. But it feels true in my head, like I deserve whatever happens to me because of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how quickly you move compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess my energy’s been pretty low. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just being lazy, because I do try to keep up.\n\nBut lately, by the time I carry one box of tile or answer a couple client messages, my body feels like wet cement. And my head gets so tired that even simple decisions feel hard. Like I’ll just sit there trying to figure out the next basic thing, and it’s… embarrassing. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that others couldn't?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah. I wasn’t sure if I should say that, because it sounds bad.\n\nSometimes I hear a man’s voice, like from behind me, at job sites or even in my kitchen. It’ll say things like, “You missed it,” or “Everyone knows.” It’s very clear, like someone is standing right there, but when I turn around there’s nobody there.\n\nI know that probably sounds… I don’t know. I’m not trying to make it bigger than it is. But yes, that has happened.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing the man's voice?\n\nPatient: I think… it’s been close to a year. Maybe around eleven months.\n\nAt first I tried to tell myself it was just stress or noise from the job site, so I didn’t really count it. But looking back, that’s about when it started… the voice, and the feeling that things were being followed through jobs or messages. I know that sounds strange. Is that the kind of timeline you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that others couldn't?\n\nPatient: Not really seeing things, no… at least not like a person or something standing there.\n\nIt’s more the voice. Like I said, it’ll sound like a man behind me, at work or sometimes in my kitchen, saying stuff like, “You missed it,” or “Everyone knows.” It feels very real in the moment, clear like someone’s right there, and then I turn around and nobody is. Is that still the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely happy, energetic, or irritable, and your thoughts were racing?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not like extremely happy or full of energy.\n\nI mean, I get irritable sometimes, sure, but it doesn’t feel like my thoughts are racing in some big way or that I’m suddenly on top of the world. I’m not sure if I’m answering that right, but I haven’t noticed a period like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely happy or on top of the world for a period of time?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had days where I felt okay or maybe a little better than usual, but not like “on top of the world” or anything extreme.\n\nIf anything, I’d notice if I suddenly had a big burst like that, I think. But no, I don’t believe that’s happened. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed or lost interest in most things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but for more than a couple weeks, probably longer, I just haven’t really cared about things I used to do.\n\nI used to paint, cook big meals, go running before jobs, show up for theater rehearsals… and now it all feels like chores from somebody else’s life. Like I know I used to enjoy them, but I don’t really feel pulled toward any of it, and I don’t care if I miss it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed or lost interest in most things that was significantly worse than what you've been experiencing lately?\n\nPatient: I guess… not really “worse than lately,” because lately has kind of been the same thing. It’s been going on for about a year and two months now, since a few months after my husband’s injury.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it, but it hasn’t just been a couple bad weeks. It’s been pretty steady, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt better, with no depression, that lasted for at least two months since your symptoms started?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had two whole months where it was just gone.\n\nThere have been weeks that were less awful, I guess, where I could function a little better or not feel quite so weighed down. But most of the time since it started, the guilt and being exhausted and not caring about things… and, um, thoughts about death, have still been there in some form.\n\nI’m sorry, I don’t mean to sound dramatic. It’s just… I don’t think there’s been a real break from it for that long.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period of feeling depressed like this in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like this. I’ve had sad times before, sure, but this one has been the main one that really stayed.\n\nIt’s been with me for about a year and two months now, since a few months after my husband’s injury. I hope that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of feeling depressed like this before this one started?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I had a period like this before. This one started a few months after my husband’s injury, and it’s just sort of stayed with me for about a year and two months now.\n\nI mean, I’ve had normal sad times before, but not something that lasted like this. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other periods in your life where you felt depressed like this before the one that started after your husband's injury?\n\nPatient: No, I really don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, like anyone, but nothing I’d put in the same category as this.\n\nThis has been the only time it felt like it really… settled in and didn’t lift. Is that the kind of distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of feeling completely better from your depression for at least two months at any point since your symptoms started?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had a stretch where I felt completely better for two months or anything like that.\n\nIt’s been kind of there the whole time, about a year and two months now, since a few months after my husband’s injury. Some days may be a little easier, but I wouldn’t say it fully went away. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of feeling depressed like this before the one that started after your husband's injury, that lasted for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: No, not that I can remember. I’ve had times where I was sad or stressed, but I don’t think anything before this lasted at least two weeks in the same way.\n\nI hope I’m answering that clearly… this one after my husband’s injury is the only one that really stands out to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’m not trying to make it bigger than it is. But yes, that has happened.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing the man's voice?\n\nPatient: I think… it’s been close to a year. Maybe around eleven months.\n\nAt first I tried to tell myself it was just stress or noise from the job site, so I didn’t really count it. But looking back, that’s about when it started… the voice, and the feeling that things were being followed through jobs or messages. I know that sounds strange. Is that the kind of timeline you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that others couldn't?\n\nPatient: Not really seeing things, no… at least not like a person or something standing there.\n\nIt’s more the voice. Like I said, it’ll sound like a man behind me, at work or sometimes in my kitchen, saying stuff like, “You missed it,” or “Everyone knows.” It feels very real in the moment, clear like someone’s right there, and then I turn around and nobody is. Is that still the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely happy, energetic, or irritable, and your thoughts were racing?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not like extremely happy or full of energy.\n\nI mean, I get irritable sometimes, sure, but it doesn’t feel like my thoughts are racing in some big way or that I’m suddenly on top of the world. I’m not sure if I’m answering that right, but I haven’t noticed a period like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely happy or on top of the world for a period of time?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had days where I felt okay or maybe a little better than usual, but not like “on top of the world” or anything extreme.\n\nIf anything, I’d notice if I suddenly had a big burst like that, I think. But no, I don’t believe that’s happened. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed or lost interest in most things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but for more than a couple weeks, probably longer, I just haven’t really cared about things I used to do.\n\nI used to paint, cook big meals, go running before jobs, show up for theater rehearsals… and now it all feels like chores from somebody else’s life. Like I know I used to enjoy them, but I don’t really feel pulled toward any of it, and I don’t care if I miss it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed or lost interest in most things that was significantly worse than what you've been experiencing lately?\n\nPatient: I guess… not really “worse than lately,” because lately has kind of been the same thing. It’s been going on for about a year and two months now, since a few months after my husband’s injury.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it, but it hasn’t just been a couple bad weeks. It’s been pretty steady, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt better, with no depression, that lasted for at least two months since your symptoms started?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had two whole months where it was just gone.\n\nThere have been weeks that were less awful, I guess, where I could function a little better or not feel quite so weighed down. But most of the time since it started, the guilt and being exhausted and not caring about things… and, um, thoughts about death, have still been there in some form.\n\nI’m sorry, I don’t mean to sound dramatic. It’s just… I don’t think there’s been a real break from it for that long.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period of feeling depressed like this in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like this. I’ve had sad times before, sure, but this one has been the main one that really stayed.\n\nIt’s been with me for about a year and two months now, since a few months after my husband’s injury. I hope that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of feeling depressed like this before this one started?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I had a period like this before. This one started a few months after my husband’s injury, and it’s just sort of stayed with me for about a year and two months now.\n\nI mean, I’ve had normal sad times before, but not something that lasted like this. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other periods in your life where you felt depressed like this before the one that started after your husband's injury?\n\nPatient: No, I really don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, like anyone, but nothing I’d put in the same category as this.\n\nThis has been the only time it felt like it really… settled in and didn’t lift. Is that the kind of distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of feeling completely better from your depression for at least two months at any point since your symptoms started?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had a stretch where I felt completely better for two months or anything like that.\n\nIt’s been kind of there the whole time, about a year and two months now, since a few months after my husband’s injury. Some days may be a little easier, but I wouldn’t say it fully went away. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of feeling depressed like this before the one that started after your husband's injury, that lasted for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: No, not that I can remember. I’ve had times where I was sad or stressed, but I don’t think anything before this lasted at least two weeks in the same way.\n\nI hope I’m answering that clearly… this one after my husband’s injury is the only one that really stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had another period of depression like this one before it started?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve been unhappy or worn down before, but not another period like this one.\n\nI hope that’s clear—this is the only time I’d really describe it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had another episode of depression like this one before this one started?\n\nPatient: I think I already kind of said, but no… I don’t believe I had another episode like this before this one started.\n\nI’ve had normal rough times, I guess, but nothing I’d put in the same category. Is that okay?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
